-e^{x}\partial_{x}&\mathrm{i}\end{array}\right)\,\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x}).

The relation for
L^{+}
-operators

\displaystyle\textstyle\left(u-v+P\right)\,\bigl(L^{+}(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes L^{+}(v)\bigr)=\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes L^{+}(v)\bigr)\,\bigl(L^{+}(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\left(u-v+P\right)

transforms to the relation for
M
-operators

\displaystyle\textstyle\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)\,\bigl(M(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes M(v)\bigr)=\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes M(v)\bigr)\,\bigl(M(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right).

after the transformation
F\cdots F^{-1}
and the necessary
change of variables and spectral parameters
u\to-\mathrm{i}u
and
v\to-\mathrm{i}v
.

B.1.3
Reduction to the Toda
L
-operators

In this section we consider reduction
from the
M
-operator for the DST-chain to
the
L
-operator for the Toda chain

\displaystyle M(u)=\begin{pmatrix}u+\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&e^{-x}\\[4.0pt]
-e^{x}\partial_{x}&\mathrm{i}\end{pmatrix}\longrightarrow L(u)=\begin{pmatrix}u+\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&e^{-x}\\[4.0pt]
-e^{x}&0\end{pmatrix}

or explicitly

e^{-\lambda x}\,M(u-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x}\xrightarrow{\lambda\to\infty}\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\,L(u).

(B.41)

Indeed we have

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda x}\,M(u-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x}=\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\[4.0pt]
0&\lambda\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u+\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&e^{-x}\\[4.0pt]
-e^{x}-\lambda^{-1}\,e^{x}\partial_{x}&\mathrm{i}\lambda^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\xrightarrow{\lambda\to\infty}\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\[4.0pt]
0&\lambda\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}u+\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&e^{-x}\\[4.0pt]
-e^{x}&0\end{pmatrix}.

The relation for
M
-operators

\displaystyle\textstyle\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)\,\bigl(M(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes M(v)\bigr)=\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes M(v)\bigr)\,\bigl(M(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)

after transformation
e^{-\lambda x}\,\cdots\,e^{-\lambda x}
and
change of the spectral parameters
u\to u-\mathrm{i}\lambda
,
v\to v-\mathrm{i}\lambda

in the limit
\lambda\to\infty
gives

\displaystyle\textstyle\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\otimes\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\,\bigl(L(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes L(v)\bigr)\,=

\displaystyle\textstyle\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\otimes\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes L(v)\bigr)\,\bigl(L(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right).

Using identity

\displaystyle\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\otimes\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\,\,\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)=\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\otimes\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)

we derive

\displaystyle\textstyle\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right)\,\bigl(L(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes L(v)\bigr)=\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes L(v)\bigr)\,\bigl(L(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,\left(u-v+\mathrm{i}P\right).

B.2
R
-operators and
RLL
-relations

In the case when
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are spaces of two
arbitrary representations of
s\ell_{2}
and
V_{3}=\mathbb{C}^{2}

the Yang–Baxter relation is reduced to the defining relation for
the general
R
-operator

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u-v)\,\mathsf{L}_{1}(u)\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(v)=\mathsf{L}_{2}(v)\,\mathsf{L}_{1}(u)\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u-v)\,,

(B.42)

where by
\mathsf{L}_{1}(u)
and
\mathsf{L}_{2}(v)
we denote the
\mathsf{L}
-operators corresponding to different representations

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{1}(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+s_{1}+z_{1}\partial_{1}&-\partial_{1}\\
z_{1}^{2}\partial_{1}+2s_{1}z_{1}&u-s_{1}-z_{1}\partial_{1}\end{array}\right),\quad\mathsf{L}_{2}(v)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}v+s_{2}+z_{2}\partial_{2}&-\partial_{2}\\
z_{2}^{2}\partial_{2}+2s_{2}z_{2}&v-s_{2}-z_{2}\partial_{1}\end{array}\right).

The equivalent form of this relation is

\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{1},v_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{1}(u_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(v_{1},v_{2})=\mathsf{L}_{1}(v_{1},v_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(u_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{1},v_{2})

(B.43)

where

\displaystyle u_{1}=u+s_{1}-1,\ u_{2}=u-s_{1};\ v_{1}=v+s_{2}-1,\ v_{2}=v-s_{2}.

(B.44)

Operator
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{1},v_{2})
interchanges pairs
of parameters
u_{1}\,,u_{2}
and
v_{1}\,,v_{2}
in the product
of
L
-operators. It can be factorized in a product of
simpler operators
[
D
,
DM
]

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{1},v_{2})=P_{12}\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(v_{1},u_{2}|v_{2})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2}),

(B.45)

where
P_{12}
is the operator of permutation

\displaystyle P_{12}\,\Psi(z_{1}\,,z_{2})=\Psi(z_{2}\,,z_{1})\,,

The operators
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})
and

\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{2})
obey the following relations

\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{1}(u_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(v_{1},v_{2})=\mathsf{L}_{1}(v_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(u_{1},v_{2})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})\,,

(B.46)

\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{1}(u_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(v_{1},v_{2})=\mathsf{L}_{1}(u_{1},v_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(v_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{2})\,.

(B.47)

Operator
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})
interchanges
parameters
u_{1}
and
v_{1}
in the product of
L
-operators and
operator
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{2})
interchanges
parameters
u_{2}
and
v_{2}
.
These operators are not independent. Indeed, using relation

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}^{-1}(u_{1},u_{2})=-(u_{1}u_{2})^{-1}\,\mathsf{L}(-u_{2},-u_{1})

(B.48)

it is easy to derive the following connection between two basic operators

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1},u_{2}|v_{2})=P_{12}\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(-v_{2}|-u_{2},-u_{1})\,P_{12}\,.

(B.49)

Below we will use the operator
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})
as the basic one.
In generic situation the solution of the defining equation (
B.46
)
can be represented in two equivalent forms, which
can be used depending on the context.
The first representation is simple and formal

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})=\frac{\Gamma(z_{21}\partial_{2}+u_{1}-v_{2}+1)}{\Gamma(z_{21}\partial_{2}+v_{1}-v_{2}+1)}\,,

(B.50)

while the second representation decodes the first one as an integral operator

\left[\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},v_{2})\Psi\right](z_{1},z_{2})=\\
\frac{1}{\Gamma(v_{1}-u_{1})}\,\int_{0}^{1}d\alpha\;\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,(1-\alpha)^{u_{1}-v_{2}}\,\Psi(z_{1},(1-\alpha)z_{2}+\alpha z_{1})\,.

(B.51)

B.3
Intertwining operators for the products of
L^{\pm}
-operators

The goal of this section is the derivation of the
intertwining relations for the products of
L^{\pm}
-operators

\displaystyle(1-z_{2}\partial_{1})^{u-v}\,\mathrm{L}^{-}_{1}(u)\,\mathrm{L}^{-}_{2}(v)=\mathrm{L}^{-}_{1}(v)\,\mathrm{L}^{-}_{2}(u)\,(1-z_{2}\partial_{1})^{u-v}\ \,,

(B.52)

\displaystyle(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{1}(u)\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{2}(v)=\mathrm{L}^{+}_{1}(v)\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{2}(u)\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\ \,,

(B.53)

using appropriate reductions.
These formal representations for intertwining operators
clearly show some of its properties.
First of all at the point
u=v
intertwining
operators reduce to the identity operator.
It is natural because for
u=v
there is
nothing to change in the defining relations.
In the case of second relation the equivalent representation
of intertwining operator as an integral operator is

\displaystyle(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{1}{\Gamma(v-u)}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v-u-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2}-\alpha z_{1})\,.

(B.54)

We derive the second relation starting from the
defining relation for the operator
R_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{1}(u_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(v_{1},u_{2})=\mathsf{L}_{1}(v_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{L}_{2}(u_{1},u_{2})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})\,.

In a first step we extract the leading asymptotics as
u_{2}\to\infty
using (
B.31
)

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{1}^{+}(u_{1})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{2}^{+}(v_{1})=\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{1}^{+}(v_{1})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L^{+}(u_{1})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})\,,

and then transform everything to the needed form

\displaystyle u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,L_{1}^{+}(u_{1})\,L_{2}^{+}(v_{1})=L_{1}^{+}(v_{1})\,L_{2}^{+}(u_{1})\,u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,

(B.55)

using relation

\displaystyle\Lambda_{+}(u)L^{+}(v)\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(u)=u^{z\partial}L^{+}(v)u^{-z\partial}\,.

(B.56)

This relation shows that for
L^{+}
-operator the
matrix similarity transformation by the matrix
\Lambda_{+}(u)

is equivalent to the of operator similarity transformation
by the dilatation operator
u^{z\partial}

\displaystyle u^{z\partial}\Psi(z)=\Psi(uz)\,.

In details and step by step we have

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{1}^{+}(u_{1})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{2}^{+}(v_{1})

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{1}^{+}(v_{1})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L^{+}(u_{1})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}\,L_{1}^{+}(u_{1})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{2}^{+}(v_{1})\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(u_{2})

\displaystyle=L_{1}^{+}(v_{1})\,\Lambda_{+}(u_{2})L_{2}^{+}(u_{1})\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(u_{2})\,\mathsf{R}_{12}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}L_{1}^{+}(u_{1})\,u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}L_{2}^{+}(v_{1})u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}

\displaystyle=L_{1}^{+}(v_{1})\,u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}L_{2}^{+}(u_{1})u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,\mathsf{R}_{12}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\mathsf{R}_{12}u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,L_{1}^{+}(u_{1})L_{2}^{+}(v_{1})

\displaystyle=L_{1}^{+}(v_{1})L_{2}^{+}(u_{1})\,u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\mathsf{R}_{12}u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,.

It remains to calculate the leading asymptotic of the operator

u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})\,u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}
when
u_{2}\to-\infty
.
The most straightforward way is to use the integral representation

\left[u_{2}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\mathsf{R}_{12}(u_{1}|v_{1},u_{2})u_{2}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}\Psi\right](z_{1},z_{2})\\[4.0pt]
=\frac{1}{\Gamma(v_{1}-u_{1})}\,\int_{0}^{1}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,(1-\alpha)^{u_{1}-u_{2}}\,\Psi(z_{1},(1-\alpha)z_{2}+u_{2}\alpha z_{1})\\[4.0pt]
=\frac{(-u_{2})^{u_{1}-v_{1}}}{\Gamma(v_{1}-u_{1})}\,\int_{0}^{-u_{2}}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,(1+\alpha u^{-1}_{2})^{u_{1}-u_{2}}\,\Psi(z_{1},(1+\alpha u^{-1}_{2})z_{2}-\alpha z_{1})\\[4.0pt]
\xrightarrow{u_{2}\to-\infty}\frac{(-u_{2})^{u_{1}-v_{1}}}{\Gamma(v_{1}-u_{1})}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2}-\alpha z_{1}).

Note that the appearing scalar factor
(-u_{2})^{u_{1}-v_{1}}

is inessential because it can be cancelled in the relation (
B.55
).
Next we transform the integral representation
to the operator form using formulae

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{\lambda-1}\,e^{-\alpha A}=\Gamma(\lambda)\,A^{-\lambda}\ \ ;\ \ e^{-\alpha z_{1}\partial_{2}}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2})=\Psi(z_{1},z_{2}-\alpha z_{1}),

so that one obtains

\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2}-\alpha z_{1})=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,e^{-\alpha z_{1}\partial_{2}}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2})\\
=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{v_{1}-u_{1}-1}\,e^{-\alpha(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2})=\Gamma(v_{1}-u_{1})\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u_{1}-v_{1}}\,\Psi(z_{1},z_{2})\,.

The formula (
B.52
) for
L^{-}
-operators
can be derived in a similar way.

B.3.1
Reduction to the DST intertwiners

Let us rewrite the relation

\displaystyle(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,L^{+}_{1}(u)\,L^{+}_{2}(v)=L^{+}_{1}(v)\,L^{+}_{2}(u)\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}

(B.57)

in a new representation as relation for
M
-operators

\displaystyle(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}\,M_{1}(u)\,M_{2}(v)=M_{1}(v)\,M_{2}(u)\,(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}\,.

(B.58)

The Fourier transformation
F_{1}F_{2}\ldots F^{-1}_{1}F^{-1}_{2}

gives

\displaystyle(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,L^{+}_{1}(u)\,L^{+}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle=L^{+}_{1}(v)\,L^{+}_{2}(u)\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle(1-z_{2}\partial_{1})^{u-v}\,\hat{L}^{+}_{1}(u)\,\hat{L}^{+}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle=\hat{L}^{+}_{1}(v)\,\hat{L}^{+}_{2}(u)\,(1-z_{2}\partial_{1})^{u-v}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{u-v}\,M_{1}(\mathrm{i}u)\,M_{2}(\mathrm{i}v)

\displaystyle=M_{1}(\mathrm{i}v)\,M_{2}(\mathrm{i}u)\,(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{u-v}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}\,M_{1}(u)\,M_{2}(v)

\displaystyle=M_{1}(v)\,M_{2}(u)\,(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}

On the first step we use

F_{1}F_{2}\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,F^{-1}_{1}F^{-1}_{2}=(1-z_{2}\partial_{1})^{u-v}

and then perform the needed change of variables and spectral parameters.
Let us perform the change of variables in a more visual way to reconstruct the obtained integral operator. We have

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v)\,(1-z_{2}\partial_{1})^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}\,\hat{\Psi}(z_{1},z_{2})=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,e^{\alpha z_{2}\partial_{1}}\,\hat{\Psi}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,\hat{\Psi}(z_{1}+\alpha z_{2},z_{2})=\int_{0}^{\infty}d\alpha\,\alpha^{\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,e^{-\alpha}\,\hat{\Psi}(z_{1}+\alpha z_{2},z_{2})

\displaystyle=z_{2}^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}\,\int_{z_{1}}^{\infty}dz\,(z-z_{1})^{\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,e^{-\frac{z-z_{1}}{z_{2}}}\,\hat{\Psi}(z,z_{2}).

Next, change the variables
z_{k}=e^{-x_{k}}\,,z=e^{-x}
and correspondingly denote
\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x_{1}},e^{-x_{2}})=\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})
,
\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x},e^{-x_{2}})=\varphi(x,x_{2})
,
so that the above formulas transform into

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v)\,(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u}\,\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})=

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{x_{1}}dx\,\,\exp\left(\mathrm{i}(u-v)(x_{2}-x)+e^{x_{2}-x_{1}}-e^{x_{2}-x}\right)\,\left(1-e^{x-x_{1}}\right)^{\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,\varphi(x,z_{2}).

Finally, for operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)
defined by equation

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,M_{1}(u)\,M_{2}(u-v)=M_{2}(u-v)\,M_{1}(u)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)

(B.59)

one obtains the following explicit formula for the
action on the function
\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})

\bigl[\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,\varphi\bigr](x_{1},x_{2})=\bigl[P_{12}\,(1+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\partial_{x_{1}})^{-\mathrm{i}v}\,\varphi\bigr](x_{1},x_{2})\\[6.0pt]
=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\mathrm{i}v)}\,\int_{-\infty}^{x_{2}}dy\;\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(x_{1}-y)-e^{x_{1}-y}+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\bigr)\,\bigl(1-e^{y-x_{2}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{i}v-1}\,\varphi(y,x_{1}).

(B.60)

Modulo normalization this is the integral operator (
A.5
) introduced in the Appendix
A
.

In Appendix
A
we also use another
\mathcal{R}
-operator (
A.14
) that intertwines
M
-operator and transposed
M
-operator

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u)\,M_{1}(u-v)=M_{1}(u-v)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u)\,\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v).

(B.61)

Let us derive the explicit expression for this operator
starting from the same relation

\displaystyle(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,L^{+}_{1}(u)\,L^{+}_{2}(v)=L^{+}_{1}(v)\,L^{+}_{2}(u)\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}\,.

First of all, transposed
L^{+}
-operator can be obtained from

L^{+}
-operator using Fourier transformation (
B.40
)

\displaystyle\left[L^{+}(u)\right]^{t}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+\partial z&-z\\
-\partial&1\end{array}\right)=F\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u-z\partial&\mathrm{i}\partial\\
\mathrm{i}z&1\end{array}\right)F^{-1}

\displaystyle=F\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\mathrm{i}&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-u-1+\partial z&-\partial\\
-z&1\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\mathrm{i}&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)F^{-1}=F\,\Lambda^{-1}\,L^{+}(-u-1)\,\Lambda^{-1}\,F^{-1}

so that

\displaystyle L^{+}(u)\,=\Lambda\,F^{-1}\,\left[L^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,F\,\Lambda\ \ ;\ \ \Lambda=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathrm{i}&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right).

By analogy with (
B.56
) there is relation

\Lambda\,L^{+}(u)\,\Lambda^{-1}=\mathrm{i}^{-z\partial}\,L^{+}(u)\,\mathrm{i}^{z\partial}

which allows to reformulate the matrix similarity transformation with the matrix
\Lambda

as the operator similarity transformation with the dilatation operator

\mathrm{i}^{z\partial}
, where

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}^{z\partial}\Psi(z)=\Psi(\mathrm{i}z)\ \ ;\ \ \mathrm{i}^{-z\partial}\Psi(z)=\Psi(\mathrm{i}^{-1}z)=\Psi(-\mathrm{i}z)\,.

We have (for brevity, denote
r_{12}=(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v}
)

\displaystyle r_{12}\,L^{+}_{1}(u)\,L^{+}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle=L^{+}_{1}(v)\,L^{+}_{2}(u)\,r_{12}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle r_{12}\,\Lambda\,F^{-1}_{1}\,\left[L_{1}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,F_{1}\,\Lambda\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{L}^{+}_{1}(v)\,\Lambda\,F^{-1}_{2}\,\left[L_{2}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,F_{2}\,\Lambda\,r_{12}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle F_{2}\,r_{12}\,F^{-1}_{1}\,\left[L_{1}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,\Lambda\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{2}(v)\,\Lambda^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Lambda^{-1}\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{1}(v)\,\Lambda\,\left[L_{2}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,F_{2}\,r_{12}\,F^{-1}_{1}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle F_{2}\,r_{12}\,F^{-1}_{1}\,\left[L_{1}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{2}(v)\,\mathrm{i}^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,\mathrm{L}^{+}_{1}(v)\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,\left[L_{2}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,F_{2}\,r_{12}\,F^{-1}_{1}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,F_{2}\,r_{12}\,F^{-1}_{1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,\left[L_{1}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,L^{+}_{2}(v)

\displaystyle=L^{+}_{1}(v)\,\left[L_{2}^{+}(-u-1)\right]^{t}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,F_{2}\,r_{12}\,F^{-1}_{1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}

so that after shift
u\to u-1
one obtains intertwining relation

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{12}\,\left[L_{1}^{+}(-u)\right]^{t}\,L^{+}_{2}(v)=L^{+}_{1}(v)\,\left[L_{2}^{+}(-u)\right]^{t}\,\hat{r}_{12}

(B.64)

where intertwining operator
\hat{r}_{12}
has the form

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{12}=\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,F_{2}(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v-1}F^{-1}_{1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,.

(B.65)

It remains to translate everything to our representation
and the first step is the Fourier transformation

F_{1}F_{2}\ldots F^{-1}_{1}F^{-1}_{2}

\displaystyle F_{1}F_{2}\,\hat{r}_{12}\,F_{1}^{-1}F_{2}^{-1}\,\left[\hat{L}_{1}^{+}(-u)\right]^{t}\,\hat{L}^{+}_{2}(v)=\hat{L}^{+}_{1}(v)\,\left[\hat{L}_{2}^{+}(-u)\right]^{t}\,F_{1}F_{2}\,\hat{r}_{12}\,F_{1}^{-1}F_{2}^{-1}\,,

(B.66)

where

\displaystyle F_{1}F_{2}\,\hat{r}_{12}\,F_{1}^{-1}F_{2}^{-1}=F_{1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,F_{2}^{2}\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v-1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,F_{1}^{-2}\,F_{2}^{-1}=

\displaystyle F_{1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,(-)^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v-1}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}\,(-)^{z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,F_{2}^{-1}\,.

In the last line we used

\left[F^{2}\,\Psi\right](z)=\Psi(-z)=(-)^{z\partial}\,\Psi(z).

To avoid misunderstanding we should note that considered operator
acts on the functions
\hat{\Psi}(z_{1},z_{2})
where
z_{1}\geq 0
and
z_{1}\geq 0
.
Let us decode obtained representation for the intertwining operator

\displaystyle\hat{\Psi}(z_{1},z_{2})\xrightarrow{F_{2}^{-1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\,e^{\mathrm{i}pz_{2}}\,\hat{\Psi}(z_{1},p)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{i}^{-z_{2}\partial_{2}}(-)^{z_{1}\partial_{1}}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\,e^{pz_{2}}\,\hat{\Psi}(-z_{1},p)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{(1+z_{1}\partial_{2})^{u-v-1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\,e^{pz_{2}}\,(1+z_{1}p)^{u-v-1}\hat{\Psi}(-z_{1},p)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{i}^{-z_{1}\partial_{1}}\,(-)^{z_{2}\partial_{2}}}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\,e^{-pz_{2}}\,(1-\mathrm{i}z_{1}p)^{u-v-1}\hat{\Psi}(\mathrm{i}z_{1},p)\xrightarrow{F_{1}}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dk\,e^{-\mathrm{i}kz_{1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\,e^{-pz_{2}}\,(1-\mathrm{i}kp)^{u-v-1}\hat{\Psi}(\mathrm{i}k,p).

Next step is the change of integration variable
\mathrm{i}k\to k
so that
we obtain the intertwiner as the following integral operator

\displaystyle\left[F_{1}F_{2}\,\hat{r}_{12}\,F_{1}^{-1}F_{2}^{-1}\,\hat{\Psi}\right](z_{1},z_{2})=(-\mathrm{i})\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{+\mathrm{i}\infty}dk\,e^{-kz_{1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{dp}{2\pi}\,e^{-pz_{2}}\,(1-kp)^{u-v-1}\hat{\Psi}(k,p).

The contour of integration over
k
is imaginary axis and by condition
z_{1}\geq 0
it is possible to deform it to the contour of Hankel type in right half-plane
along to the branch cut from the point
k=\frac{1}{p}\geq 0
to the
k=+\infty
.
For the values above and below real axis we have

(1-(k\pm\mathrm{i}0)p)^{u-v-1}=|1-kp|^{u-v-1}\,e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(u-v-1)},

so that

(1-(k+\mathrm{i}0)p)^{u-v-1}-(1-(k-\mathrm{i}0)p)^{u-v-1}=2\mathrm{i}\,\sin(\pi(u-v))\,|1-kp|^{u-v-1}.

After all one obtains

\displaystyle\left[F_{1}F_{2}\,\hat{r}_{12}\,F_{1}^{-1}F_{2}^{-1}\,\hat{\Psi}\right](z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{\sin(\pi(u-v))}{\pi}\,\int_{0}^{\infty}dp\,e^{-pz_{2}}\,\int_{\frac{1}{p}}^{+\infty}dk\,e^{-kz_{1}}\,|1-kp|^{u-v-1}\,\hat{\Psi}(k,p).

In order to obtain intertwiner

\displaystyle P_{12}\,\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(u-v)\,\left[M_{1}(-u)\right]^{t}\,M_{2}(v)=M_{1}(v)\,\left[M_{2}(-u)\right]^{t}\,P_{12}\,\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(u-v)\,,

(B.67)

it remains to change spectral parameters
u\to-\mathrm{i}u
and
v\to-\mathrm{i}v
, pass to the exponential variables
z_{1}=e^{-x_{1}}
,
z_{2}=e^{-x_{2}}
and

k=e^{-y_{1}}
,
p=e^{-y_{2}}
, and switch to the function

\varphi(x_{1}\,,x_{2})=\hat{\Psi}\left(e^{-x_{1}}\,,e^{-x_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\left[P_{12}\,\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(u-v)\varphi\right](x_{1},x_{2})=\frac{\sin(\pi(\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u))}{\pi}\,

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dy_{2}\,e^{-y_{2}}\,e^{-e^{-y_{2}-x_{2}}}\,\int_{-y_{2}}^{-\infty}dy_{1}\ e^{-y_{1}}\,e^{-e^{-y_{1}-x_{1}}}\,|1-e^{-y_{1}-y_{2}}|^{\mathrm{i}v-\mathrm{i}u-1}\,\varphi(y_{1},y_{2}).

At last, to obtain intertwiner

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,\left[M_{1}(-u)\right]^{t}\,M_{2}(u-v)=M_{1}(u-v)\,\left[M_{2}(-u)\right]^{t}\,\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,,

(B.68)

we have to multiply by permutation
P_{12}
from the left (i.e. interchange
x_{1}\leftrightarrows x_{2}
), replace
v\to u-v
and slightly rewrite
the whole integral representation

\left[\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\varphi\right](x_{1},x_{2})=\frac{\sin(\mathrm{i}\pi v)}{\pi}\,\int{R}dy_{2}\,\int_{y_{2}}^{\infty}dy_{1}\,\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(y_{2}-y_{1})-e^{-x_{1}-y_{2}}-e^{y_{1}-x_{2}}\bigr)\\[6.0pt]
\times(1-e^{y_{2}-y_{1}})^{-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,\varphi(-y_{1},y_{2}).

Up to coefficient behind the integral this is exactly the formula (
A.14
).

B.3.2
Reduction to the Toda intertwiners

Let us derive the integral operator
\mathcal{R}_{12}(v)
intertwining
Toda and DST Lax matrices

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{12}(v)\,L_{1}(u)\,M_{2}(u-v)=M_{2}(u-v)\,L_{1}(u)\,\mathcal{R}_{12}(v)

(B.69)

from the integral operator
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)
that
intertwines two DST Lax matrices

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,M_{1}(u)\,M_{2}(u-v)=M_{2}(u-v)\,M_{1}(u)\,\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v).

(B.70)

We perform the shifts
v\to v-\mathrm{i}\lambda
and

u\to u-\mathrm{i}\lambda
in the last relation and rewrite
everything in a suitable form

e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}M_{1}(u-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,\,M_{2}(u-v)\\
=M_{2}(u-v)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}M_{1}(u-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x_{1}}.

Taking the asymptotics as
\lambda\to\infty
we obtain

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)L_{1}(u)\,\,M_{2}(u-v)=M_{2}(u-v)\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)L_{1}(u)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x_{1}}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,L_{1}(u)\,M_{2}(u-v)=\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(\lambda)M_{2}(u-v)\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)\,L_{1}(u)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x_{1}}.

The identity

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(\lambda)\,M(u)\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)=e^{-(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x}}\,M(u)\,e^{+(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x}}

(B.71)

allows to rewrite the obtained relation in a needed form

\displaystyle e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,L_{1}(u)\,\,M_{2}(u-v)=M_{2}(u-v)\,L_{1}(u)\,e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x_{1}}.

The action of the obtained operator on the function is defined
by the following explicit formula

e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)\,e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})\sim\\[4.0pt]
e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\,\int_{-\infty}^{x_{2}}dy\;\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)(x_{1}-y)-e^{x_{1}-y}+e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\bigr)\,\bigl(1-e^{y-x_{2}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{i}(v-\mathrm{i}\lambda)-1}\,e^{\lambda y}\,\varphi(y,x_{1})=\\[4.0pt]
\int_{-\infty}^{x_{2}+\ln\lambda}dy\;\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(x_{1}-y)-e^{x_{1}-y}+\lambda^{-1}\,e^{x_{1}-x_{2}}\bigr)\,\bigl(1-\lambda^{-1}\,e^{y-x_{2}}\bigr)^{\mathrm{i}v-1+\lambda}\,\varphi(y,x_{1})\\[4.0pt]
\xrightarrow{\lambda\to\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dy\;\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(x_{1}-y)-e^{x_{1}-y}-e^{y-x_{2}}\bigr)\,\varphi(y,x_{1})=\bigl[\mathcal{R}_{12}(v)\,\varphi\bigr](x_{1},x_{2}).

In a similar way we derive the integral operator

\mathcal{R}^{*}_{12}(v)
that permutes Toda matrix with transposed DST matrix

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{*}_{12}(v)\,M^{t}_{2}(-u-v)\,L_{1}(u)=L_{1}(u)\,M^{t}_{2}(-u-v)\,\mathcal{R}^{*}_{12}(v)

(B.72)

from the
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)
-operator that intertwines
DST and transposed DST Lax matrices

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,M_{1}(u)=M_{1}(u)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v).

First, we rewrite the last relation in appropriate equivalent form

e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}M_{1}(u-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\\
=e^{-\lambda x_{1}}M_{1}(u-\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}

and then consider the asymptotics as
\lambda\to\infty

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)L_{1}(u)=\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)L_{1}(u)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(\lambda)M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,\Lambda_{+}(\lambda)L_{1}(u)=L_{1}(u)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}

After using the same relation (
B.71
) one obtains

e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,L_{1}(u)\\
=L_{1}(u)\,M_{2}^{t}(-u-v)\,e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}.

The action of the obtained operator on the function is defined
by the following explicit formula

e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})=\\
e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\int{R}dy_{2}\,\int_{y_{2}}^{\infty}dy_{1}\,\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)(y_{2}-y_{1})-e^{-x_{1}-y_{2}}-e^{y_{1}-x_{2}}\bigr)\\[6.0pt]
\times(1-e^{y_{2}-y_{1}})^{-\mathrm{i}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)-1}\,e^{-\lambda y_{1}}\varphi(-y_{1},y_{2}+\ln\lambda)=\\
\lambda^{\lambda-iv}\,\int{R}dy_{2}\,\exp-\lambda\bigl(y_{2}+x_{1}+e^{-x_{1}-y_{2}}\bigl)\int_{y_{2}-\ln\lambda}^{\infty}dy_{1}\,\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(y_{2}-y_{1})-e^{y_{1}-x_{2}}\bigr)\\[6.0pt]
\times(1-\lambda^{-1}e^{y_{2}-y_{1}})^{-\mathrm{i}v-1+\lambda}\,\varphi(-y_{1},y_{2}).

The leading asymptotic contribution as
\lambda\to\infty
is obtained by
the application of the standard Laplace method

\displaystyle\int{R}dy_{2}\,f(y_{2})\,e^{\lambda\,S(y_{2})}\to f(\bar{y}_{2})\,e^{\lambda\,S(\bar{y}_{2})}\,\sqrt{-\frac{2\pi}{\lambda S^{\prime\prime}(\bar{y}_{2})}}

where
\bar{y}_{2}
is obtained from equation
S^{\prime}(\bar{y}_{2})=0
.
In our example

S(y_{2})=-\bigl(y_{2}+x_{1}+e^{-x_{1}-y_{2}}\bigl)
so that
\bar{y}_{2}=-x_{1}
and

e^{-\lambda x_{1}}\widehat{\mathcal{R}}_{12}(v+\mathrm{i}\lambda)e^{\lambda x_{1}}\,e^{(\ln\lambda)\partial_{x_{2}}}\varphi(x_{1},x_{2})\xrightarrow{\lambda\to\infty}\\
\lambda^{\lambda-\mathrm{i}v}\,\sqrt{\frac{2\pi}{\lambda}}\,\int_{\mathbb{R}}dy_{1}\,\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(-x_{1}-y_{1})-e^{y_{1}-x_{2}}-e^{-x_{1}-y_{1}}\bigr)\,\varphi(-y_{1},-x_{1}).

This expression coincides up to normalization factor and change
of variables
y_{1}\to-y
with the formula (
1.69
)

\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{R}^{*}_{12}(v)\,\varphi\bigr](x_{1},x_{2})=\int_{\mathbb{R}}dy\;\exp\bigl(\mathrm{i}v(y-x_{1})-e^{y-x_{1}}-e^{-y-x_{2}}\bigr)\,\varphi(y,-x_{1}).

B.4
Reflection equation

The general reflection equation associated with the XXX spin chain has the following form
[
Skl2
]

\displaystyle\mathsf{R}_{12}(u-v)\,\mathsf{K}_{1}(u)\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}_{2}(v)=\mathsf{K}_{2}(v)\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}_{1}(u)\,\mathsf{R}_{12}(u-v)\,.

(B.73)

Operator
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u)
acts in the tensor product
V_{1}\otimes V_{2}

of two representations of
s\ell_{2}
with spins
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
.
It is the function of spectral parameter
u
and the spins which determine the representations.
Reflection operator
\mathsf{K}_{1}(u)
is defined in the space
V_{1}
of representation with spin
s_{1}
, and the similar operator
\mathsf{K}_{2}(v)
acts in the space
V_{2}
corresponding to spin
s_{2}
.

In the full analogy with Yang–Baxter equation there are different variants
of the general reflection equation depending on the choice of
representations
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

In the simplest case of two-dimensional representations
V_{1}=V_{2}=\mathbb{C}^{2}
the operator
\mathsf{R}_{12}(u)
degenerates into the finite-dimensional solution of Yang–Baxter equation — the Yang’s
R
-matrix acting
in the tensor product
\mathbb{C}^{2}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{2}

R_{12}(u)=R(u)=u+P\,,\qquad P\,a\otimes b=b\otimes a\,.

The reflection relation (
B.73
) is reduced in this case to the form

R(u-v)\,\bigl(\widehat{K}(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,R(u+v)\,\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes\widehat{K}(v)\bigr)\\[4.0pt]
=\bigl(\bm{1}\otimes\widehat{K}(v)\bigr)\,R(u+v)\,\bigl(\widehat{K}(u)\otimes\bm{1}\bigr)\,R(u-v)\,,

(B.74)

where
\bm{1}
is a
2\times 2
identity matrix.
It is equation for the
2\times 2

\widehat{K}
-matrix and
the general solution
[
Skl2
,
IS
]
of this relation has the following form

\displaystyle\widehat{K}(u)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{i}\alpha&u\\[6.0pt]
-\beta^{2}\,u&\mathrm{i}\alpha\end{pmatrix}.

(B.75)

In the case
V_{1}=\mathbb{C}^{2}
and
V_{2}

is the space of arbitrary representation of
s\ell_{2}
,
one obtains the defining relation for the general
reflection operator
\mathsf{K}_{2}(v)=\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)

\displaystyle\textstyle\mathsf{L}(u-v+\frac{1}{2})\,\widehat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v+\frac{1}{2})\,\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)=\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v+\frac{1}{2})\,\widehat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u-v+\frac{1}{2})\,.

After the shift of spectral parameter
u\to u-\frac{1}{2}
the defining relation takes a more convenient form

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(u-v)\,\hat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)=\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v)\,\hat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u-v)\,,

(B.76)

where we use a notation for the reflection
K
-matrix which differs from the canonical one (
B.75
) by the shift of
argument
u\to u-\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\hat{K}(u)=\textstyle\widehat{K}(u-\frac{1}{2})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{i}\alpha&u-\frac{1}{2}\\[6.0pt]
-\beta^{2}\bigl(u-\frac{1}{2}\bigr)&\mathrm{i}\alpha\end{pmatrix}.

(B.77)

The operator
\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)
is constructed explicitly as an integral operator in
[
ABDK
]

and is given by the following formula

\left[\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)\,\Phi\right](z)=\frac{(2i\beta)^{-2v}}{\Gamma(-2v)}\,(z+i\beta)^{g+v-s}\\
\times\int_{0}^{1}dt\;(1-t)^{-2v-1}\,t^{g+v+s-1}\,\left(t(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)+2\mathrm{i}\beta\right)^{v+s-g}\,\Phi(t(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)+i\beta)

(B.78)

where
g=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\alpha}{\beta}
.
Our next goal is the derivation of the following relation

L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v)\\
=\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v)\,\sigma_{2}\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,,

(B.79)

where the operator
\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v)
is obtained by the appropriate
reduction from the operator
\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}\xrightarrow{s\to+\infty}\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v)=(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g+v}\,(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{v-g+1}\,\mathrm{i}^{z\partial}\,F

(B.80)

and
\sigma_{2}\,,\sigma_{3}
are standard Pauli matrices

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&-\mathrm{i}\\
\mathrm{i}&0\end{array}\right)\ \ \ ;\ \ \ \sigma_{3}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&-1\end{array}\right)\,.

In the formula (
B.80
)
P
is the operator of inversion

\displaystyle\left[P\,\Psi\right](z)=z^{-2s}\,\Psi\left(\frac{1}{z}\right)\ \ ;\ \ P^{2}=1\,,

(B.85)

and
F
is the Fourier transformation.
As we will show on the next step the
relation (
2.2
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(v)\,M^{t}(-u-v)\,K(u)\,\sigma_{2}M(u-v)\sigma_{2}=M(u-v)\,K(u)\,\sigma_{2}M^{t}(-u-v)\sigma_{2}\;\mathcal{K}(v).

is exactly the relation (
B.79
) rewritten in an equivalent
representation. In this way one obtains the independent
derivation of the operator
\mathcal{K}(v)
.

Let us derive (
B.79
) starting from the relation

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(u-v)\,\hat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)=\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v)\,\hat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u-v).

(B.86)

The first step is inversion
P
which induces
the following transformation of generators and the whole
L
-operator

\displaystyle P\,S\,P=-S\ \ ;\ \ P\,S^{\pm}\,P=S^{\mp}\ \ ;\ \ P\,\mathsf{L}(u)\,P=\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\mathsf{L}(u)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}.

(B.87)

Indeed we have

P\,\mathsf{L}(u)\,P=P\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+S&S_{-}\\
S_{+}&u-S\end{array}\right)\,P=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u-S&S_{+}\\
S_{-}&u+S\end{array}\right)\\
=\sigma_{2}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+S&-S_{+}\\
-S_{-}&u-S\end{array}\right)\,\sigma_{2}=\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+S&S_{+}\\
S_{-}&u-S\end{array}\right)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}.

Let us multiply (
B.86
) by operator of inversion from the right

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(u-v)\,\hat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)\,P=\mathsf{K}(v\,,s)\,P\,P\,\mathsf{L}(u+v)\,P\,\hat{K}(u)\,P\,\mathsf{L}(u-v)\,P,

then use (
B.87
) and perform the shift of the spectral
parameter
v\to v+s

\mathsf{L}(u-v-s)\,\hat{K}(u)\,\mathsf{L}(u+v+s)\,\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P=\\
\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\mathsf{L}(u+v+s)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\mathsf{L}(u-v-s)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}

Now we extract the leading asymptotic contribution as
s\to\infty

\Lambda_{+}(2s)\,\sigma_{3}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\Lambda_{-}(2s)\,\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P=\\[4.0pt]
\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\Lambda_{-}(2s)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\Lambda_{+}(2s)\,\sigma_{3}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}

(B.88)

using formulae
(
B.36
)

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}(u+s)\xrightarrow{s\to\infty}L^{-}(u)\,\Lambda_{-}(2s)\ \ ;\ \ \mathsf{L}(u-s)\xrightarrow{s\to\infty}\Lambda_{+}(2s)\,\sigma_{3}\,L^{+}(u-1)\,,

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{-}(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)\ \ ;\ \ \Lambda_{+}(u)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&u\end{array}\right)\,.

Next, we multiply relation (
B.88
) by the matrix
\sigma_{3}\,\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(2s)
from the left and by the matrix
\Lambda^{-1}_{-}(2s)
from the right

L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P=\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,\sigma_{3}\,\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(2s)\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\Lambda_{-}(2s)\,\\
\times\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\Lambda_{+}(2s)\,\sigma_{3}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\Lambda^{-1}_{-}(2s).

Then transform the right hand side using

\displaystyle\sigma_{3}\,\Lambda_{\pm}(2s)=\Lambda_{\pm}(2s)\,\sigma_{3}\ \ ;\ \ \sigma_{2}\,\Lambda_{\pm}(u)=\Lambda_{\mp}(u)\,\sigma_{2}

(B.89)

and standard formulae for Pauli matrices

\sigma_{2}^{2}=\sigma_{3}^{2}=\bm{1}
,
\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}=-\sigma_{3}\sigma_{2}
, which gives

L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P=\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,\sigma_{2}\,\Lambda^{-1}_{-}(2s)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\Lambda_{-}(2s)\,\\
\times\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,\Lambda_{+}(2s)\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(2s)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}.

The last step is the use of the relations

\displaystyle\Lambda^{-1}_{-}(2s)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\Lambda_{-}(2s)=(2s)^{z\partial}\,L^{-}(u+v)\,(2s)^{-z\partial}

\displaystyle\Lambda_{+}(2s)\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\Lambda^{-1}_{+}(2s)=(2s)^{z\partial}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,(2s)^{-z\partial}

so that

L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P=\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,\sigma_{2}\,(2s)^{z\partial}\,L^{-}(u+v)\,(2s)^{-z\partial}\,\,\\
\times\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,(2s)^{z\partial}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,(2s)^{-z\partial}\,\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3},

or equivalently

L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}=\\
\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}\,\sigma_{2}\,L^{-}(u+v)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,L^{+}(u-v-1)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}.

After all one obtains relation (
B.79
) where

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}\xrightarrow{s\to\infty}\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v).

It remains to calculate explicitly the leading asymptotics for the
intertwining operator

\displaystyle\left[\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}\,\Psi\right](z)=\frac{(2\mathrm{i}\beta)^{-2v-2s}}{\Gamma(-2v-2s)}\,(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g+v}

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}dt\,(1-t)^{-2v-1}\,t^{g+v-1}\,\left(t(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)+2\mathrm{i}\beta\right)^{v-g}\,\left(\frac{t}{1-t}\frac{t(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)+2\mathrm{i}\beta}{t(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)+\mathrm{i}\beta}\right)^{2s}\,\Psi\left(\frac{2s}{t(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)+\mathrm{i}\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{2s-2v}(2\mathrm{i}\beta)^{-2v-2s}}{2s\Gamma(-2v-2s)}\,(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g+v}\,(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{v-g+1}

\displaystyle\int_{-\mathrm{i}\frac{2s}{\beta}}^{\frac{2s}{z}}dx\,\left(1-\frac{zx}{2s}\right)^{-2v-1}\,\left(1-\frac{\mathrm{i}\beta x}{2s}\right)^{g+v-1}\,\left(1+\frac{\mathrm{i}\beta x}{2s}\right)^{v-g}\,\left(\frac{\left(1+\frac{\mathrm{i}\beta x}{2s}\right)\left(1-\frac{\mathrm{i}\beta x}{2s}\right)}{1-\frac{zx}{2s}}\right)^{2s}\,\Psi(x),

so that up to overall inessential normalization coefficient one obtains

\displaystyle\left[\mathsf{K}(v+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}\,\Psi\right](z)\xrightarrow{s\to\infty}(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g+v}\,(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{v-g+1}\,\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dx\,e^{zx}\,\Psi(x).

The last step is to rewrite
everything in appropriate representation.
The procedure is very similar to the calculations
starting from the formula (
B.40
).
We have

\displaystyle F\,L^{+}(u)\,F^{-1}=F\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+\partial_{z}z&-\partial_{z}\\
-z&1\end{array}\right)\,F^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u-z\partial_{z}&-\mathrm{i}z\\
-\mathrm{i}\partial_{z}&1\end{array}\right)=\hat{L}^{+}(u)\,,

\displaystyle F\,L^{-}(u)\,F^{-1}=F\,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&-\partial_{z}\\
z&u-z\partial_{z}\end{array}\right)\,F^{-1}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&-\mathrm{i}z\\
\mathrm{i}\partial_{z}&u+\partial_{z}z\end{array}\right)=\hat{L}^{-}(u)\,.

The second step is the change of variables

\hat{L}^{+}(u)\,\hat{\Psi}(z)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u-z\partial_{z}&-\mathrm{i}z\\
-\mathrm{i}\partial_{z}&1\end{array}\right)\,\hat{\Psi}(z)\\
\longrightarrow(-\mathrm{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathrm{i}u+\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&e^{-x}\\
-e^{x}\partial_{x}&i\end{array}\right)\,\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x})=(-\mathrm{i})\,M(\mathrm{i}u)\,\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x})

and using the general formula

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}a&b\\
c&d\end{array}\right)\sigma_{2}=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}d&-c\\
-b&a\end{array}\right)

we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{2}\,\hat{L}^{-}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{\Psi}(z)=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u+\partial_{z}z&-\mathrm{i}\partial_{z}\\
\mathrm{i}z&1\end{array}\right)\,\hat{\Psi}(z)\longrightarrow(-\mathrm{i})\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\mathrm{i}u+\mathrm{i}-\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&-e^{x}\partial_{x}\\
-e^{-x}&\mathrm{i}\end{array}\right)\,\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}-\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i}+\mathrm{i}\partial_{x}&-e^{x}\partial_{x}\\
e^{-x}&\mathrm{i}\end{array}\right)\,\sigma_{3}\,\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x})=\mathrm{i}\,M^{t}(-\mathrm{i}u-\mathrm{i})\,\sigma_{3}\,\hat{\Psi}(e^{-x}).

Note that after the needed change of the spectral parameter
u\to-\mathrm{i}u

in
K
-matrix (
B.77
) one obtains exactly
K
-matrix (
1.56
)

\displaystyle\hat{K}(-\mathrm{i}u)=\begin{pmatrix}\mathrm{i}\alpha&-\mathrm{i}u-\frac{1}{2}\\[6.0pt]
-\beta^{2}\bigl(-\mathrm{i}u-\frac{1}{2}\bigr)&\mathrm{i}\alpha\end{pmatrix}=(-\mathrm{i})\begin{pmatrix}-\alpha&u-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\\[6.0pt]
-\beta^{2}\bigl(u-\frac{\mathrm{i}}{2}\bigr)&-\alpha\end{pmatrix}=(-\mathrm{i})\,K(u).

(B.90)

Using obtained formulae we rewrite the relation (
B.79
) (with shift
v\to v-1
) and operator
\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v)
in appropriate representation

\displaystyle L^{+}(u-v)\,\hat{K}(u)\,L^{-}(u+v-1)\,\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v-1)=

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v-1)\,\sigma_{2}\,L^{-}(u+v-1)\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,L^{+}(u-v)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle(-\mathrm{i})\,M(\mathrm{i}(u-v))\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,\mathrm{i}\,M^{t}(-\mathrm{i}(u+v-1)-\mathrm{i})\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\mathcal{K}(\mathrm{i}v)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\mathrm{i}v)\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{2}\,\mathrm{i}\,M^{t}(-\mathrm{i}(u+v-1)-\mathrm{i})\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}\,\sigma_{2}\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,(-\mathrm{i})\,M(\mathrm{i}(u-v))\,\sigma_{2}\,\sigma_{3}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle M(\mathrm{i}(u-v))\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,M^{t}(-\mathrm{i}(u+v))\,\sigma_{2}\,\mathcal{K}(\mathrm{i}v)=\mathcal{K}(\mathrm{i}v)\,M^{t}(-\mathrm{i}(u+v))\,\hat{K}(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,M(\mathrm{i}(u-v))\,\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle M(u-v)\,\hat{K}(-\mathrm{i}u)\,\sigma_{2}\,M^{t}(-u-v)\,\sigma_{2}\,\mathcal{K}(v)=\mathcal{K}(v)\,M^{t}(-u-v)\,\hat{K}(-\mathrm{i}u)\,\sigma_{2}\,M(u-v)\,\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle\downarrow

\displaystyle M(u-v)\,K(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,M^{t}(-u-v)\,\sigma_{2}\,\mathcal{K}(v)=\mathcal{K}(v)\,M^{t}(-u-v)\,K(u)\,\sigma_{2}\,M(u-v)\,\sigma_{2}

where the operator
\mathcal{K}(v)
is obtained in a two steps

\displaystyle\mathsf{K}(v-1+s\,,s)\,P\,(2s)^{z\partial}\xrightarrow{s\to\infty}\hat{\mathcal{K}}(v-1)=(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g+v-1}\,(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{v-g}\,\mathrm{i}^{z\partial}\,F,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(v)=F\,(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\mathrm{i}^{-z\partial}\,F\,F^{-1}=F\,(z+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(z-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\mathrm{i}^{z\partial}\,.

Let us represent the operator
\mathcal{K}(v)
explicitly as an integral operator

\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{K}(v)\,\hat{\Psi}b\bigr](z)=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dp\,e^{-\mathrm{i}pz}\,(p+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(p-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\mathrm{i}^{p\partial_{p}}\hat{\Psi}(p)

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{+\infty}dp\,e^{-\mathrm{i}pz}\,(p+\mathrm{i}\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(p-\mathrm{i}\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\hat{\Psi}(\mathrm{i}p)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{i}^{2\mathrm{i}v}\,\int_{-\mathrm{i}\infty}^{+\mathrm{i}\infty}dk\,e^{-kz}\,(k-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(k+\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\hat{\Psi}(k).

Remember that
z\geq 0
. The contour of integration over
k
is imaginary axis and by condition
z\geq 0
it is possible to close this contour in right half-plane. Due to the function
(k-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}
we have
branch cut along the positive real axis from

k=\beta\geq 0
to
k=+\infty
.
For the values above and below real axis we have

(k\pm\mathrm{i}0-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}=|k-\beta|^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,e^{\mp\mathrm{i}\pi(g-\mathrm{i}v-1)}

so that discontinuity is

(k+\mathrm{i}0-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}-(k-\mathrm{i}0-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}=2\mathrm{i}\,\sin(\pi(g-\mathrm{i}v))\,|k-\beta|^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}.

After all one obtains

\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{K}(v)\,\hat{\Psi}\bigr](z)=2\mathrm{i}\,\sin(\pi(g-iv))\,\mathrm{i}^{2iv}\,\int_{\beta}^{+\infty}dk\,e^{-kz}\,(k-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(k+\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\hat{\Psi}(k).

It remains to change variables
z=e^{-x}
,

k=e^{-y}
and switch to the function

\varphi(x)=\hat{\Psi}\left(e^{-x}\right)

\displaystyle\bigl[\mathcal{K}(v)\,\varphi\bigr](x)=-2\mathrm{i}\,\sin(\pi(g-\mathrm{i}v))\,\mathrm{i}^{2\mathrm{i}v}\,\int_{-\ln\beta}^{-\infty}dy\,e^{-y}\,e^{-e^{-x-y}}\,(e^{-y}-\beta)^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(e^{-y}+\beta)^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\varphi(y)

\displaystyle=-2\mathrm{i}\,\sin(\pi(g-\mathrm{i}v))\,\mathrm{i}^{2\mathrm{i}v}\,\int_{-\ln\beta}^{-\infty}dy\,\exp\left(2\mathrm{i}vy-e^{-x-y}\right)\,(1-\beta e^{y})^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(1+\beta e^{y})^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\varphi(y)

\displaystyle=2\mathrm{i}\,\sin(\pi(g-\mathrm{i}v))\,\mathrm{i}^{2\mathrm{i}v}\,\int_{\ln\beta}^{\infty}dy\,\exp\left(-2\mathrm{i}vy-e^{y-x}\right)\,(1-\beta e^{-y})^{g-\mathrm{i}v-1}\,(1+\beta e^{-y})^{-\mathrm{i}v-g}\,\varphi(-y)

so that up to overall normalization one obtains
the reflection operator (
1.65
).
